Potential algorithm:
```
def optimize(a0, n, time_budget, sym=True):
    w = log(a0+eps)
    m,v,t=Adam
    best=a0,best_score
    beta_schedule: 5, 20, 50, 150, 400, 1000? With smoothed logsumexp.
    for each beta phase:
       for many steps until time:
         compute c, mx, score
         weights p=softmax(beta*(c-mx)) but if c has negative small due FFT, clip.
         grad=2*conv_suffix(p, A_rev)
         grad_w=a*(grad-dot(a,grad))
         step Adam w
         if sym: a=sym(a) normalize? w = log(a)
         score = exact FFT score; accept if improved on current? Use no acceptance? Adam can be non-monotonic. We can maintain cur a and best. If score worse than cur by threshold, revert.
       increase beta.
    random restarts: from best add noise to w, run short.
```

Polynomial square flattening. We can represent sequences as **discrete convolutions of basic windows**. Nonnegative filters with flat square are related to **flat-top windows** in sampling: A window's Fourier transform squared flat over a band. But here time-domain coefficients of A^2 should be flat over support 2n-1. Equivalent to A(z) being approximating square root of a boxcar in time. In frequency domain, the boxcar length 2n-1 has DFT magnitude |sin(ω n)/sin(ω/2)|. Square root is |sin(ω n)/sin(ω/2)|^{1/2} e^{i phase}; coefficients positive? This is like sqrt of Dirichlet kernel. Could be close to some known window. But optimization numeric easier.

Maybe use **frequency parametrization**: We need A_k such that inverse of A_k^2 is flat and coefficients of A are nonnegative. A_k = DFT(a). For real nonnegative a, A_k = A_{-k}^*. Let A_k = r_k e^{iφ_k}, r_k≥0, φ_{-k}=-φ_k. Then c_k = IFFT(A_m^2) = IFFT(r_m^2 e^{i2φ_m}). We want c_j≈const. This is like synthesizing a sequence c with prescribed magnitude r^2 and phase 2φ, and then requiring inverse DFT of A (amplitudes r, phases φ) nonnegative. Could optimize r,φ with constraints? Hard but Fourier param may be faster? N up to 65536; variables N/2. Nonnegativity in time. Could use soft constraints. But log-space time params are direct.

We can implement score and gradient:
```
def conv_and_score(a, N):
  A = rfft(a,N)
  c = irfft(A*A,N)[:L]
  c = clip(c,0,None)
  mx = c.max()
  score = 2*n*mx
  return A,c,score
```
Weights:
```
def weights_for_c(c, mx, mode, beta, tau_frac):
  if mode softmax: p = exp(beta*(c-mx)); p/=sum
  elif mode hinge: d=c-mx*(1-tau_frac); p=np.where(d>0,d,0); p/=sum
  elif mode reciprocal: p=1/(c+eps) maybe
```
Hinge weights could be uniform top if tau_frac small. For high precision, use `p = exp(beta*(c - mx))` includes all but top. β maybe 50*n? Wait c-mx difference scale. c~1/n. To weight top within δ/n, use β ~ 1000/n? Let's scale: if c values ~2e-4, differences near max maybe 1e-6, β*diff ~20. β ~20e6? That is large. But weights computed as exp(beta*(c-mx)), c-mx negative. Underflow okay. β can be 1e5-1e7. Need stable: use c normalized by mx? `x=(c-mx)/mx` in [-1,0], β. β up to 1000. So use β scaled to normalized max. Good. Use `β` 50-1000.

For softmax, `p = exp(β*(c-mx)/mx_safe); p/=p.sum()`. If β large, p may one-hot; use `logsumexp` stable: shift by max diff=0. Since already shifted by mx, all ≤0; no overflow, but underflow. okay. If all c tiny? c sum 1.

Alternatively **top uniform**: find values ≥ mx - tol, p=1/count. For gradient of max at local optimum with many near equal, top uniform good. Use adaptive tol: e.g., tol = max(1e-5, mx*0.01) initially, shrink to 1e-6. Count maybe dozens. This avoids exponential instability. Could use both: `p = topKuniform` if count>1 else hinge.

Grad computation for arbitrary p:
```
P = rfft(p,N)
q = irfft(P * phase * conj(A), N)[n-1:2n-1]
grad = 2*q
```
Check if p sum=1? If p sum=1, grad integral? Not important. For Adam, grad magnitude maybe p-weighted. If top uniform p sum=1, q average ~1/(2n), grad~1/n. Good.

Log-space Adam with **step control**:
- `grad_w = a*(grad - sum(a*grad))`. Since `a` sum1. The dot term ensures sum zero in probability simplex. For symmetric, we may symmetrize after softmax.
- Use Adam: m=β1m+(1-β1)g; v=...; step=lr * mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps). Clip step to ±max_step (e.g., 0.5). New w = w - step. Then `a_new = softmax(w)`; if sym enforce mirror w? We can symmetrize w = (w + w_rev)/2 after step, then softmax. Or sym a. Sym w keeps parameters symmetric. Before optimization, set w symmetric. On update, mirror step? We can just after step set `w_new = 0.5*(w_new + w_new[::-1])`; then softmax. This enforces symmetry. Need be careful: If we mirror w after Adam, the gradient not projected exactly, but okay.
- Acceptance: Compute score_new. If finite and score_new < cur_score - tol (e.g., 1e-12) accept; else maybe try smaller step (0.5*); if none, reduce lr, keep current, but still update Adam? Usually if rejected, don't update m/v? Could update with step? Simpler: for each candidate step multiplier factor in [1,0.5,0.25,0.1] find best; if best improves current, accept with corresponding w (after clipping). If none, set lr *=0.8 and reset? This multiplies cost. Maybe use Adam automatically. We can use "proximal" line search: after Adam step, compute score; if worse than current by >1e-10, step factor 0.5 up to 3 times; if still worse, reject and reduce lr. Keep m/v for rejected? ### Algorithm A: Smoothed max descent with top active set

def optimize_smooth(a_init, budget, N, phase, max_n, sym=True):
    a = sym_norm(a_init)
    w = np.log(a + eps)
    wt = sym(w)
    m,v=zeros; t=0
    best=a; best_score=score(a)
    cur=a; cur_score=best_score
    lr=0.12
    beta=50
    no_improve=0
    start=time
    while time<deadline:
       A,c,score = conv_score(a,N,n)
       if score<best_score: best=a; best_score=score; no_improve=0
       top_count = max(4, min(64, int(0.2*n?)))? Actually number near max maybe small.
       # choose p:
       mx=c.max()
       # If beta phase: p=softmax(beta*(c-mx)/mx)
       # else top uniform: threshold = mx*(1-thr), thr decreases with time.
       p = make_weights(c,mx,step_count)
       grad = 2*IRFFT(rfft(p,N)*phase*conj(A)) slice
       grad_w = a*(grad - dot(a,grad))
       # Adam step
       for fac in [1,0.5,0.25,0.1]:
           dn = compute adam with lr*fac? Adam state if trying multiple? Can't easily.
       new_w = w - lr*adam_delta
       sym new_w
       new_a = softmax(new_w)
       new_score=score(new_a,N,n)
       if new_score < cur_score - 1e-14:
           w=new_w; a=new_a; cur_score=new_score; best update; lr*=1.03

           # try half step perhaps by saving original? Could do after computing new_a for factor 1, if fail compute 0.5 from same delta
       # every X steps adjust beta/thr
```

### Algorithm B: Deconvolution flattening
```
def deconvolve_flat(a, N, phase, target='flat'|'inv', iters=50, mix=0.5):
    b=a.copy()
    A=rfft(a,N); U=phase*conj(A)
    if target flat: t = 1/(2n-1)
    else t based on y
    for it in range(iters):
       B=rfft(b,N)
       y=irfft(A*B,N)[:L]; y=max(y, eps)
       if target flat:
          r = t / y
       else: # inverse current c? maybe t = 1/(c+eps) normalized? 
       P=rfft(r,N)
       corr=irfft(P*U,N)[n-1:2n-1]
       # multiply b
       b_new = b * corr
       # normalize, maybe sym
       s=b_new.sum()
       if s>0: b_new/=s
       b = (1-mix)*b + mix*b_new
       sym
    return b
```
But if a fixed, b update converges to solution of a*b ≈ t; when a=b, square flat. We can then set a=mix(old a,b). In main loop, after some deconv iters, evaluate a_new = (1-τ)a + τ b; if score improves accept. Then continue gradient. This may be a powerful non-gradient move.

Target t: uniform 1/L. But if current y has zeros, r huge. Use `eps = max(y)*1e-12`. Since all positive initialized, y positive. Also normalize r maybe: `r = r / mean(r)`? In deconv update, if r all large, corr scale affects b before normalization; only relative matters. But corr sum maybe not 1. We can normalize baseline: `r = (t / y); r /= np.mean(r)` to avoid huge. Good.

Could also use **multiplicative self-square update**: Use y=a*a, t=flat, r=t/y, corr=A^T r, a_new=a*corr, normalize. This is a one-step direct update. Iterate several times with mix. Let's test fixed point: if y=t, r=1, corr=S=1, a_new=a. Good. This avoids separate b and maybe directly drives square flat. However if y has very small values, r huge. Clip. This is like **DAMM**? We can implement both: direct deconv of a with target. This may be more stable and cheaper (no b updates? same cost). We'll call it `self_deconvolve(a, t, iters, mix)`.

At each iteration:
- y=conv(a,a)
- r = t / max(y, eps); r /= mean(r)
- corr = A^T r (using A for current a? But after updating a, A changes; recompute each iteration)
- a_new = a * corr; normalize; sym
- a = mix(a, a_new)

### Active-set LP / cross-subproblem revisited
Could improve by using the parent SOTA's LP idea correctly. Let's derive a descent method using two sequences.
We want reduce max of a*a. Let current a (sum1). Let active coefficients c_a(c). For direction d (sum0), derivative of c_k: 2(a*d)_k - 2 c_k? Wait c(t) = (a+t d)^2 = a^2 +2t a*d + t^2 d^2. Derivative at 0 =2(a*d)_k. We don't need -c unless direction mean? If we choose d = b - a, derivative =2(a*b)_k -2c_k. To reduce active max M, need a*b_k < c_k=M. So we want b such that a*b active < current c. And sum b=1. This is feasible if minimum over b≥0,sum1 of max_active a*b_k < M. Since b=a gives a*b=a*a, max_active=M. Need strictly lower; if no, at optimum. This is an LP: minimize T subject a*b_k≤T for k in active set, sum b=1,b≥0. If optimal T < M, moving to b reduces active. We can find b (maybe by multiplicative weights) and mix. This is more targeted than minimizing all a*b. The previous SOTA LP maximizing sum b subject a*b≤M is the dual feasibility: if we can find b sum1 with a*b≤M? That derivative ≤2M not negative. Not enough. But the minimizing T active is useful.

How to solve this LP approximately?
Primal: min T s.t. ∑_{i} a_i b_{k-i} ≤ T for k in A (valid), ∑b=1,b≥0. This is a linear program. The matrix has K rows, n cols. For K maybe 20-100. Could solve exactly with `scipy.optimize.linprog` using high-res simplex? 5000 vars, 100 constraints; each call maybe seconds. But we can do many? Maybe final polish: solve for active top 8 coefficients, get b, mix. This could break local minima.

Alternatively, find b via **Nash equilibrium** (minimax): minimize max_{k∈A} (B_k^T b). This is a zero-sum game; we can use multiplicative weights on b_i:
- Maintain y (dual) over active K.
- At each iter, b = argmin_i? Actually b distribution over actions i, row player (minimizer) chooses b_i. Payoff for b,k is (A b)_k. Equilibrium satisfies all i in support minimize weighted payoff ∑_k y_k (A b)_k? Update y to put mass on k with maximum current payoff (or softmax), update b_i *= exp(-η ∑_k y_k A_{k i}). This is the same as optimizing smooth max of active a*b. This is previous cross_inner but restricted to active and perhaps with subtraction c_k? We want a*b active < c. If we minimize a*b absolute, b may be feasible. We can then mix.

We can implement active minimax cross-subproblem:
```
def find_b_active(a, A, active_indices, iters, beta):
  b = a.copy()
  # For each active k, cost vector cost_i = a_{k-i} (valid). This is gradient q_k without 2.
  # Objective F(b)= max_{k active} a*b_k. Smooth weights y over active based on current F.

  # Use Adam.
```
Then candidate a_new = (1-t)a + t b for t=0.25,0.5,1. Evaluate. If improves, accept. The cross update may be more stable if active set small. This matches SOTA's use of g0 optimizing f*g0 ∞; but they did all lags and maximize sum? We can do active. Since objective max of a*a, reducing active derivatives is good. Inactive can increase up to M maybe.

We can combine direct self-gradient and cross-active. The direct self-gradient is the derivative of self objective; cross-active finds b to minimize first-order active. - At current a, c, mx.

Let's compare to direct self-gradient: Direct gradient with weights p on active is equivalent to one gradient descent step for objective with p; cross b optimization is solving min_b ∑? Actually direct gradient uses p = weights on c and gradient =∑ p_k 2a_{k-j}. Cross subproblem at b=a with same p has gradient =∑ p_k a_{k-j}, half. Then Adam step on b is akin moving a directly but with an extra optimization of b; the b after inner is the result of several gradient steps on linear model. This can take larger effective steps.

We should implement both but focus cross active.

**LP alternative with scipy**: For final polishing, we can solve the active minimax LP exactly to get b. Since K small, variables n large. `scipy.optimize.linprog(c, A_ub, ...)`. But n=5k, K=32; maybe too slow? We can formulate dual with K+1 variables and n constraints? Let's see:
Primal: min t
s.t. (A_active b)_k - t ≤ c_k? Actually minimize max(pay) = min t s.t. (A_active b)_k - c_k ≤ t, sum b=1,b≥0. Bounded t can be negative. Dual: max -∑ c_k y_k + v? s.t. y≥0, ∑ y_k=1? Let's derive not needed. The dual has K variables y and one v, constraints for each b_i: ∑_k y_k a_{k-i} - v ≤ 0 (since b_i≥0). v free? Could solve via finding y such that row minimum? Not simpler. But maybe use cvxpy with PDLP for K=10, n=5000? Could be slow. We'll avoid.

**Active set selection**: Need ensure max coefficient included. If multiple near equal, include all above `mx*(1-tol)`. If tol too small, K=1; optimize one coefficient may overshoot. If tol too large, K large, direction averages. Use adaptive tol: start 0.005 (0.5% below max), shrink to 0.0005. For n large, coefficients differ; many may be within. We can also include top min(Kmax, count). Kmax maybe 32-128. If K too large, FFT P dense; same cost. Use Kmax=64 for speed. If all within tolerance >64, use top 64. If K=1, maybe use top 8 with intensity? We can select K = max(8, min(64, number within 0.2%)). This focuses top. Need direct score improvement may require controlling all maxima, but if another coefficient becomes max later, next outer updates active.

**Beta for cross weights**: Scale pay by mx. β 50-500. Since pay values can be O(mx). Use β=100.

**Line search for mix t**: Values: [0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0]? Candidate a_new=(1-t)a + t b. If t=1, new sequence b; derivative active negative but b self may be bad. Start with smaller t (0.25). We can choose best hard score. Since b is optimized for first-order, t=1 may still improve. Use try [0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0]. Compute scores (4 FFTs). Accept best if < current. If no, inner fail; maybe adjust inner lr.

To save time, we can evaluate t=0.5 then 0.25 etc. But okay.

**Inner iterations**: For each outer cross, maybe 8-20 steps. Estimate cost: each inner step B rfft, hib irfft, P rfft, G irfft; 4 FFT transforms. For n=5k N=16k, each maybe 0.3ms? 4*10=12 transforms ~ maybe 10ms. 1000 outer ->10s? Actually numpy rfft 16k ~0.2-0.5ms, irfft similar, so 4*0.4=1.6ms, times 12=19ms per outer, 1000=19s? Maybe rfft 16k = 0.1ms? Fine. For N=65k maybe 1-2ms, 4=8ms*12=100ms outer, 100=10s. Feasible.

Direct self-gradient uses 2 rfft/irfft per step; could be faster. Cross uses 4. Use both: priority cross for structured improvement, self for local.

**Normalization and scaling**: Work sum=1. Parameters w log a. For cross subproblem, b is independent variable in simplex; use its own log w_b, Adam. After b updated, maybe symmetrize b as well. If a symmetric, to keep candidate symmetric, sym b. The cross optimization over all b may break symmetry; symmetrizing b may alter objective a*b? Since a symmetric and objective active set symmetric? If active set selection includes symmetric pairs, the minimizer can be symmetrized without increasing max? Likely. We'll sym b after each step to enforce.

**Current vs best state**: Main loop can run cross active until stall, then direct self-gradient, then deconv, then restart.

Potential algorithm structure:
```
def optimize(a_init, budget, target_score=1.5027):
  n=len(a); N=next_fast_len(3n-2); phase=...
  a=sym_norm(a_init); w=log(a)
  best=a; best_score=score
  curr=a; curr_score=best_score
  b=a; w_b=log(b); adam states for b and self
  lr_b=0.1; lr_self=0.05; β=100; inner=8; tol=5e-3
  start=time
  stall=0
  outer=0
  while time < deadline:
     A,c,score = fft_score(a)
     if score<best_score: update
     if time > 0.8 budget: break for final
     # choose strategy based on outer/stall
     if outer % 3 == 0 or stall>...: cross_active
     else: self_gradient
     # every 10 outer: deconv flat (a few)
     # if stall high: restart perturb
  return best
```
But each outer should be adaptive. Cross active changes a; after accept, reset b=a and inner state. Self gradient changes a; reset b. Deconv changes a; reset.

**Self gradient with active top**: Use p weights on current c. Gradient formula. Adam state w_a. This is similar to cross but one step. Could be used when cross stalls.

**Deconv target**: Use `self_deconvolve(a, iterations=20, mix=0.3, target_type='flat')`. Evaluate final a; accept if improves current/best. This is a large step. Could run after cross blocks.

**Random perturbation**: If cross fails 20 times, take best, add Gaussian noise to w (sym) with σ; run a short local optimize. We can implement by resetting curr to noisy and continuing loop. Keep best.

**Multiple lengths**: Main will call optimize on candidate sequences. But optimize itself lengthy. We need final code manageable.

### Helper: next_fast_len
```
def next_fast_len(m):
    if m<=1: return 1
    p=1
    while p < m: p <<=1
    return p
```
Could use 3*n-2. For n=1? But n large.

### Helper: make_soft_pos
```
def make_probability(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float).ravel()
    if v.size==0: return np.ones(1)
    v=np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    v=np.maximum(v,0.0)
    s=v.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: v=np.ones(v.size); s=v.size
    return v/s
```

### softmax_log
```
def weight_from_log(w):
   w=np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, -1e300)
   w=w - max(w)
   e=np.exp(w)
   s=e.sum()
   if s<=0: uniform
   return e/s
```
For n large, w differences can be large; okay.

### sym symmetrize

def symmetrize(a):

    return 0.5*(a + a[::-1])

def symmetrize_w(w): return 0.5*(w + w[::-1])

### score_fft
```
def fft_score(a,N):
   A=rfft(a,N)
   c=irfft(A*A,N)
   c=c[:2*a.size-1]
   c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
   mx=c.max()
   score=2*n*mx
   return A,c,mx,score
```
Return A for reuse. Sum 1 assumed.

### weights
```
def soft_top_weights(c, mx, beta):
   if m<=0: uniform
   # use normalized by mx to avoid extreme beta? If mx>0:
   x=(c-mx)/mx
   x=np.where(x>0,0,x) # due round
   z=beta*x
   # clip z to -745 to avoid underflow
   z=np.clip(z, -745, 0)
   p=np.exp(z)
   s=p.sum()
   if s>0: return p/s
   # fallback max uniform
   idx=np.argmax(c); p=np.zeros_like(c); p[idx]=1; return p
```
But if β large, p sum could underflow if top few not exactly max due to c max shifted 0, exp(0)=1, so sum ≥1. Good. Use c-mx maybe 0 for argmax. If max repeated, p multiplicity.
Top uniform alternative:
```
def top_uniform_weights(c, mx, k, rel_tol=1e-3):
   threshold = mx*(1-rel_tol)
   idx = np.where(c >= threshold)[0]
   if len(idx) > k:
       # take largest k? sorted
       idx = idx[np.argsort(c[idx])[-k:]] # may not be stable but okay
   if len(idx)==0: idx=[], argmax
   p=np.zeros_like(c); p[idx]=1/len(idx)
   return p
```
Sorting O(L log K). Use `np.argpartition` for top k if large:

if len(idx)>k: idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
```

```

### gradient from weights p

def grad_from_weights(A, P, phase, N, n):
   q=irfft(P * (phase * np.conjugate(A)), N)
   grad = 2.0 * q[n-1:2*n-1]
   return grad
```

### Adam step with log w
```
def adam_log_step(w, grad, m, v, t, lr, max_step=1.0):
   t +=1
   m = 0.9*m +0.1*grad
   v = 0.999*v +0.001*grad*grad
   mh = m/(1-0.9**t)
   vh = v/(1-0.999**t)
   delta = lr * mh/(np.sqrt(vh)+1e-12)
   delta=np.clip(delta,-max_step,max_step)
   w_new = w - delta
   # nan safe
   w_new=np.where(np.isfinite(w_new), w_new, w)
   # maybe center to prevent large
   w_new = w_new - np.max(w_new)
   return w_new, m, v, t
```
But for candidates with factor, we need delta before update. Return delta and updated state? If reject, state had update. Simpler function returns delta, m, v,t (state updated). Then test w - mult*delta.
```
def adam_delta(grad,m,v,t,lr,max_step): ... return delta,m,v,t
```

When using sym w, after w_new and sym, we might need to recenter. Good.

### softmax from w with sym

def w_to_a(w):
   if sym: w=0.5*(w+w[::-1])
   w=np.where(finite,w,-1e300)
   w -= max(w)
   e=exp(w); s=e.sum(); if s>0 a=e/s else uniform
   return a
```
For very low entries, underflow to 0; okay but then grad_w zeros for those indices, may get stuck. To keep positive, we can floor? If we use softmax, some entries may become exactly zero (when w diff > ~745). That could be bad because zero entries cannot be increased through grad_w (a_i factor) in log gradient. But Adam can update w_i even if a_i=0? grad_w = a_i*(...) =0, so no. Thus we must maintain all entries positive enough. Underflow to zero happens if log differences > ~745. We can clip w differences to e.g., [-50, 0] inside w_to_a to maintain min ~1e-22 relative. Use `w = np.minimum(0, np.maximum(w - max(w), -50))`. This floors relative prob ~1.9e-22; for n=5k, sum still 1. This prevents zero. But may hinder sparsity. Optimal likely all positive moderate; floor okay. Use floor -40 or -50. If n=16384, min 1e-15? -30 relative 9e-14; enough. Let's use FLOOR_LOGW = -50 maybe too tiny; nan? exp(-50)=1.9e-22, sum n*1e-22 negligible. Better use -30 (9e-14) to keep contributions. But if optimal requires very small, floor may affect. Target probabilities ~1e-3, not tiny. Use -40 (4e-18) safe. The gradient for tiny a negligible. Could use -60. Let's use -50.

### Cross-active implementation detailed
```
def cross_active_step(a, A, c, n, N, phase, b, w_b, m_b, v_b, t_b, lr_b, beta, inner, tol, kmax, sym=True):
   L=2*n-1
   mx=c.max()
   # active indices
   thr = mx*(1-tol)
   idx = np.where(c >= thr)[0]
   if idx.size < 1: idx=np.array([np.argmax(c)])
   if idx.size > kmax:
      idx = np.argpartition(c[idx], -kmax)[-kmax:]
   # if idx.size==1 maybe add neighboring? Could use k=8 top:
   if idx.size < 8:
      # choose top 8 unique
      idx = np.argpartition(c, -min(8,L))[:min(8,L)]
   # inner state
   base_c_active = c[idx]
   for it in range(inner):
       B = rfft(b,N)
       h = irfft(A * B, N)[:L]
       h = np.maximum(h,0)
       pay = h[idx] - base_c_active
       # smooth weights over active:
       maxpay = pay.max()
       # if maxpay not finite, uniform
       z = beta * (pay - maxpay) / mx
       z = np.clip(z, -50, 0)
       y = exp(z); s=y.sum(); if s>0: y/=s else uniform
       P = zeros(L); P[idx]=y
       Pfft = rfft(P,N)
       G = irfft(Pfft * (phase*conj(A)), N)[n-1:2n-1]  # no factor
       # log gradient for b
       gw = b * (G - np.dot(b, G))
       delta,m_b,v_b,t_b = adam_delta(gw,m_b,v_b,t_b,lr_b,0.5)
       w_b_new = w_b - delta
       if sym: w_b_new=0.5*(w_b_new+w_b_new[::-1])
       b_new = softmax(w_b_new)
       # Optionally evaluate b's own score? not needed.
       b=b_new; w_b=w_b_new
   return b
```
Note: `phase*conj(A)` computed once outside; but in this function A fixed, so we can pass `U = phase*conj(A)` to avoid recompute. For deconv and self, U_a changes if a changes. We manage.

Potential issue: If b is symmetric and A symmetric? The cost G computed for active idx not symmetric; the log gradient may break symmetry; we sym b after step, but objective may increase. That's okay.

After inner, line search:
```
for tval in (0.35,0.65,1.0,0.2,0.8):
 cand=(1-t)*a + t*b
 cand=sym_norm(cand)
 score=fft_score(cand)
 keep best if < current
```
Need compute A/c for candidate? Score function does. Could use if candidate sum 1.

If accepted, update a=cand, w_a=log(cand), reset b=a, clear m_b/v_b/t_b? Yes.

If not accepted, maybe reduce lr_b or increase inner? Should not too heavy. We'll maintain `lr_b` adaptive: if accepted, lr_b*=1.1 up to 0.4; else *=0.85 down to 0.02.

### Self gradient step

def self_step(a, A, c, mx, w_a, m_a, v_a, t_a, lr_a, beta, sym=True):
   p = soft_top_weights(c,mx,beta)  # sum1
   P=rfft(p,N)
   U=phase*conj(A)
   G=2*irfft(P*U,N)[n-1:2n-1]
   gw = a*(G - dot(a,G))
   delta,... = adam...
   w_new=w_a - delta
   sym
   a_new=softmax(w_new)
   score=fft_score(a_new)
   accept if improves current; if not, maybe try smaller delta by recomputing? We'll use if score < current + 1e-12? For monotonic try accepting if < current. If not, reduce lr.
```
But Adam state already updated; if reject and later use same, okay. We can keep current w.

### Deconv self update

def deconvolve_update(a, A, c, N, phase, iters, mix, target_mode):
   b=a.copy()
   L=2*n-1
   for it in range(iters):
       B=rfft(b,N)
       y=irfft(A*B,N)[:L]  # a*b, if b current? Actually for direct square, use b=b? We can use b as iterate to compute b*b? But fixed point a? Let's use direct self with b current: y=conv(b,b). If target t fixed, update b.
       y=max(y, eps)
       if target_mode flat: t=1/L
       elif clipped: t = min(c, M_target); normalize  # c initial? maybe y
       r=t/y; r/=mean(r)
       P=rfft(r,N)
       # gradient/correlation with b? For direct self fixed point constant y=b*b, update b_i *= (B^T r)_i / sum? Need kernel b itself? If using convolution b*b, derivative of y wrt b_j =2b_{?}. Multiplicative update b_new = b * corr(t/y, b). Use U=phase*conj(rfft(b)) for current b. But if we use a fixed kernel a, update b such that a*b target. For direct, use b as kernel.
       Bfft=rfft(b,N)
       U=phase*conj(Bfft)
       corr=irfft(P*U,N)[n-1:2n-1]
       b_new=b*corr
       normalize
       b=(1-mix)*b + mix*b_new
   return b
```

**Self deconv fixed point**:
```
def deconv_self(a, N, phase, iters, mix, target_type, target_m):
    b=a.copy()
    L=2*n-1
    for it in range(iters):
        B=rfft(b,N)
        y=irfft(B*B,N)[:L]; y=np.maximum(y, eps)
        if target_type == 0:
            t = 1.0/L
        elif target_type == 1:
            t = np.minimum(y, target_m)
            s=t.sum()
            if s>0: t/=s
            else: t=1/L
        elif target_type == 2:
            # inverse y
            t = 1.0 / np.maximum(y, eps)
            s=t.sum(); t/=s
        else:
            # window
            center = (L-1)/2
            w = np.cos(np.pi * np.abs(np.arange(L)-center)/center) # 1 center 0 edges? Actually cos pi=0.
            t = w; t=np.maximum(t,0.1*w.max())? Normalize and scale? We want max maybe target_m? For target as guide, relative shape; normalize sum1.
        r = t / y
        r = r / np.mean(r)
        P=rfft(r,N)
        U=phase*conj(B)
        corr=irfft(P*U,N)[n-1:2n-1]
        corr=np.maximum(corr,0)
        b_new = b * corr
        s=b_new.sum()
        if s>0: b_new/=s
        else: b_new=b
        if mix<1: b_new = (1-mix)*b + mix*b_new
        b = sym_norm(b_new)
    return b
```

   remaining = m_clip - t
   capacity = remaining.sum()

### Possible mathematical initialization via deconv from flat

       update global best

    # final remaining: optimize current global best (with its region/sym) for chunk3

    # final exact validate top candidates, choose best exact (within time)
    # finalize scale sqrt(2n)
    return list
```

### Let's think of more global algorithm: Particle swarm / CMA-ES on low-dimensional Fourier coefficients
Could use to escape local minima. But full-dim random restart sufficient maybe.

**Shape prior via low-frequency noise**: Restart perturbations with full noise may make sequence rough and score high; local descent slow. Use smooth Fourier perturbations:
```
def smooth_noise_w(n, rng, sigma):
   w=np.zeros(n)
   for r in range(1,6):
      amp=rng.normal(0,sigma/(r+1))
      ph=rng.uniform(0,2π)
      x=np.linspace(0,1,n,dtype=float)
      w += amp*np.cos(2π*r*x+ph)
   w += rng.normal(0,sigma*0.1,n)
   return sym(w)
```
This keeps global shape. Use in restarts.

### More on cross-active descent
At each outer, after b inner, line search with mix. What if b is close to a but score not improve? We can try **anti-direction**? If derivative active negative but inactive coefficients increase causing max, maybe a mix too small? If small t not improve, larger worse? Then no feasible direction in active minimizer? Could use self-gradient.

The cross subproblem minimizes max pay over active. The optimal value v* may be >=0 if no b can reduce active first-order. Then current is first-order optimum for active set. If not all coefficients active, maybe need include more. If v*≥0 for a set of all top, local optimum. If optimal, direct self gradient no improve. Need global restart.

We can monitor `max_pay` after inner. If `max_pay > -1e-6*mx`, cross not useful. Then maybe set active tol larger/kmax more next outer.

### Could use **barycentric average of best candidates** (mirror descent)
Maintain best_a and current; if local stall, take convex combination of top candidates: `a = softmax(mean(log a1 + log a2))`? Might produce new shape. We can implement simple: combine best with a random top candidate via log interpolation: w = 0.5 log(a)+0.5 log(b). Normalize. Evaluate. This can explore basin. Add when stall.

#### normalize_prob
```
def normalize_prob(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/float(a.size)
    return a/s
```

#### sym
```
def symmetrize(a):
    return 0.5*(a + a[::-1].copy())
```

#### normalize_prob_given_sym
```
def normalize_sym(a, sym=True):
    a=normalize_prob(a)
    if sym:
       a=symmetrize(a)
       a=normalize_prob(a)
    return a
```
Maybe symmetrize after normalization slightly changes sum; normalize again.

#### log_from_prob
```
def log_from_prob(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    mn = 1e-12
    a=np.maximum(a,mn)
    w=np.log(a)
    w=np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0.0)
    w = 0.5*(w+w[::-1])? maybe not; caller sym.
    maxw=w.max()
    floor=maxw - 50.0
    w=np.maximum(w,floor)
    w-=maxw
    return w
```
If a has zeros from underflow, floor to 1e-12 may not be enough for n large? 1e-12*n sum small. Use `mn = max(1e-18, 1e-300)`? 1e-18, log -41, above floor -50. Good. Use `mn = 1e-15` maybe. Let's set `TINY = 1e-15`.

#### prob_from_log
```
def prob_from_log(w, sym=True):
    w=np.asarray(w,float).ravel()
    if sym: w=0.5*(w+w[::-1])
    w=np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, -100.0)
    maxw=float(np.max(w))
    floor=maxw - 45.0
    w=np.maximum(w,floor)
    w-=maxw
    e=np.exp(w)
    s=e.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: a=np.ones(w.size)/w.size
    else: a=e/s
    if sym: a=symmetrize(a); a=normalize_prob(a)
    return a
```
The floor prevents zero. Note if w contains large negative before floor, set to max-45.

#### fft_score_info
```
def fft_score_info(a, N, n):
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)
    L = 2*n -1
    c = c[:L]
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c,0.0)
    # normalize? Since a sum 1, sum c should 1; FFT tiny errors. Could renormalize c? The objective uses coefficients from convolution, not normalized c. But numerical sum may be 0.999999. Scaling c by 1/sum may correct? If we renormalize c to sum1, score changes small. For consistency, do not? Could improve max error. Since a sum1, sum(c)=1. If FFT error sum=1+eps, max maybe eps relative. We can optionally `s=c.sum(); if s>0: c = c/s`. This enforces sum and may reduce error, but changes exact objective by ~1e-15. Safe. Do it? It may hide errors but fine. Let's do if finite and s>0 and s<2: c=c/s.
    mx=float(c.max())
    score=2.0*n*mx
    return A,c,mx,score
```
But if we renormalize c, gradient cross offset c must align with score max. It's okay. For line search exact hidden not renormalized but errors tiny.

#### soft_top_weights
```
def soft_top_weights(c, mx, beta=100.0, uni_mix=0.2):
    L=c.size
    if mx <= 0 or not finite: return uniform
    x=(c-mx)/mx
    x=np.minimum(x,0.0)
    # if beta infinite? clip
    z=np.clip(beta*x, -60.0, 0.0)  # -60 enough; exp(-60)<1e-26
    p=np.exp(z)
    s=p.sum()
    if s <=0:
       idx=np.argmax(c); p=np.zeros(L); p[idx]=1
    else: p/=s
    if uni_mix>0:
       p = (1-uni_mix)*p + uni_mix/L
       s=p.sum(); if s>0: p/=s
    return p
```
Using -60 may undercount coefficients >60/log beta below max; good. If beta=0, uniform. For self, uni_mix maybe 0.1.

#### top_uniform_weights maybe not needed if beta low. For cross, use active weights with uniform mix.

#### adam_delta
```
def adam_delta(grad, m, v, t, lr, max_step=1.0):
    grad=np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    t_new = t + 1
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999
    m_new = m*beta1 + (1-beta1)*grad
    v_new = v*beta2 + (1-beta2)*grad*grad
    mhat = m_new/(1 - beta1**t_new)
    vhat = v_new/(1 - beta2**t_new)
    delta = lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-12)
    delta = np.where(np.isfinite(delta), delta, 0.0)
    delta = np.clip(delta, -max_step, max_step)
    return delta, m_new, v_new, t_new
```

#### cross_active_inner
Signature:
```
def cross_active_inner(a, A, U, c, b, w_b, m_b, v_b, t_b, lr, beta, inner, active_tol, kmax, mix_y, N, n, sym=True):
    L=2*n-1
    mx=float(c.max())
    if mx <=0: active all zeros
    thr=mx*(1.0-active_tol)
    idx=np.where(c >= thr)[0]
    if idx.size ==0: idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
    # ensure include argmax
    amax=int(np.argmax(c))
    if amax not in idx: idx=np.append(idx,amax)
    if idx.size > kmax:
        # keep top kmax by c
        # Use argpartition on c values of idx
        vals=c[idx]
        kk=min(kmax, idx.size)
        part=np.argpartition(vals, -kk)[-kk:]
        idx=idx[part]
    # If idx small (<4), add top 4 unique maybe
    if idx.size < 4:
        kk=min(4,L)
        idx=np.argpartition(c, -kk)[-kk:]
    base=c[idx]
    maxpay_initial=0.0
    for it in range(inner):
        B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
        h=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)[:L]
        h=np.where(np.isfinite(h),h,0.0)
        h=np.maximum(h,0.0)
        pay=h[idx]-base
        maxpay=float(np.max(pay)) if pay.size else 0
        # store
        maxpay_initial=maxpay
        # weights over active
        if pay.size:
            # if maxpay > -0.5*mx? use softmax, else uniform maybe
            z=(pay - maxpay)/mx
            # If maxpay very negative, softmax over negative pay still selects least negative; okay.
            z=np.clip(beta*z, -60.0, 0.0)
            y=np.exp(z); s=y.sum()
            if s>0: y/=s
            else: y=np.ones(pay.size)/pay.size
            if mix_y>0:
                y = (1.0-mix_y)*y + mix_y/pay.size
                y/=y.sum()
        else: y=np.ones(1)
        P=np.zeros(L)
        P[idx]=y
        Pfft=np.fft.rfft(P,N)
        G=np.fft.irfft(Pfft*U,N)[n-1:2*n-1]
        G=np.where(np.isfinite(G), G, 0.0)
        gw=b*(G - float(np.dot(b,G)))
        if sym:
            gw=0.5*(gw+gw[::-1])
        delta,m_b,v_b,t_b=adam_delta(gw,m_b,v_b,t_b,lr,0.5)
        w_new=w_b - delta
        b_new=prob_from_log(w_new,sym=sym)
        b=b_new
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
            b=a.copy(); w_b=log_from_prob(a); break
    return b, w_b, m_b, v_b, t_b, maxpay_initial
```
Need `amax in idx` check O(K) fine. `P` allocated each inner; could be heavy. We can allocate inside; okay. To reduce, if idx same across inner, P buffer from outside? Not necessary.

After inner, `maxpay_initial` maybe max pay after last? For use, to know if direction reduces. If final maxpay <0. Could return final maxpay. But pay decreases? Use final. In loop set `last_maxpay`. Return it.

```
def find_best_mixin(a,b,base_score,N,n,sym=True,tuple):
   best_a=None; best_score=base_score
   for t in tuple:
      cand=(1-t)*a+t*b
      cand=normalize_sym(cand,sym)
      sc=fft_score_only(cand,N,n)
      if sc < best_score - 1e-14:
          best_score=sc; best_a=cand
   return best_a,best_score
```
If none, returns None,base.

#### self_gradient_step
```
def self_gradient_step(a, A, U, c, mx, w_a, m_a, v_a, t_a, lr, beta, N, n, sym=True):
    p=soft_top_weights(c,mx,beta=beta,uni_mix=0.15)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    G=2.0*np.fft.irfft(P*U,N)[n-1:2*n-1]
    G=np.where(finite,G,0)
    gw=a*(G-dot(a,G))
    if sym: gw=0.5*(gw+gw[::-1])
    delta,m_a,v_a,t_a=adam_delta(gw,m_a,v_a,t_a,lr,0.5)
    w_new=w_a - delta
    a_new=prob_from_log(w_new,sym=sym)
    return a_new,w_new,m_a,v_a,t_a
```
Then line search with current? Self step already a_new; we can evaluate and accept/reject. We can also try scale multiplier if fails.

#### deconv_self_candidate
Implement direct multiplicative update as above. Use `A_cur`? It updates b, so A changes each iteration. Signature:
```
def deconv_self_candidate(a, N, phase, iters, mix, target_type, target_m, n, sym=True):
    b=a.copy()
    L=2*n-1
    for it in range(iters):
       B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
       y=np.fft.irfft(B*B,N)[:L]; y=max(y,eps)
       m=max(y)
       if target_type==0:
           t=1.0/L
       elif target_type==1:
           t=make_clipped_target(y, target_m)
       elif target_type==2:
           # shape center
           k=np.arange(L); cidx=(L-1)/2
           shape=1.0 - 0.7*((k-cidx)/cidx)**2 if cidx>0 else 1
           shape=np.maximum(shape,0.05)
           t=shape / shape.sum()
           # optionally scale to max target? If target_m >0: if max(t)>target_m? But normalized sum1; max small. Keep.
       else:
           t=1.0/L
       r=t/y
       mr=np.mean(r)
       if mr>0: r=r/mr
       P=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
       U=phase*np.conjugate(B)
       corr=np.fft.irfft(P*U,N)[n-1:2*n-1]
       corr=np.maximum(corr,0)
       b_new=b*corr
       s=b_new.sum()
       if s>0: b_new/=s
       b_new=(1-mix)*b + mix*b_new
       b=normalize_sym(b_new,sym)
    return b
```
For target_type 1 clipped, use `target_m = max(target_score/(2n), m*0.95?)` maybe. But if target_m very small and y max huge? make_clipped_target handles.
`make_clipped_target`:
```
def make_clipped_target(y, m_cap):
    L=y.size
    t=np.minimum(y, m_cap)
    s=t.sum()
    if s <=0:
       return np.ones(L)/L
    if s < 1.0:
       deficit=1.0-s
       room=m_cap - t
       room=np.maximum(room,0)
       cap=room.sum()
       if cap > deficit:
           t += (deficit/cap)*room
       else:
           # add to all up to cap, then uniform? if cap insufficient (target_m*L <1), fallback flat
           t = np.ones(L)/L
           return t
    s=t.sum()
    if s <=0: return np.ones(L)/L
    t=t/s
    # enforce cap? after scaling if sum>1 scale down, max unchanged maybe
    t=np.minimum(t,m_cap*1.01)??
    s=t.sum(); if s>0:t/=s
    return t
```
If target_m*L >=1, cap sufficient. Good.

### optimize_candidate details implementation
We need a time limit. Use `budget_s`; if <=0 return initial.

```

    b=a.copy(); w_b=log_from_prob(b); m_b=zeros; v_b=zeros; t_b=0

    beta_cross=80; beta_self=120
    active_tol=4e-3; kmax=32; inner_iters=10; mix_y=0.5
    stall=0; outer=0
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    top=[(score,a.copy())]
    last_print=start
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
       if not finite...
       A,U,c,mx,cur_score...? We need A,U current. At top compute if cur changed? We can keep A_cur, U_cur. Let's compute at start of each outer: A_cur,c_cur,mx_cur,cur_score_new=fft_score_info(cur,N,n); if cur_score_new < cur_score? update. U_cur=phase*conj(A_cur). This costs one score per outer. Cross inner line search also scores; total okay. We can use cur_score from previous? For active set need c. So compute.
       # update best
       # choose move
       # To balance, use counter: cross when outer%3!=2, self when outer%3==2? That is 2 cross, 1 self. deconv every 25.
       if outer % 25 == 20: deconv
       elif outer % 3 == 2: self
       else: cross
```
But cross inner already includes B/h and P; self similar. If cross fails often, outer continues.

**Cross acceptance and state update**:
```
if move cross:
   b_new, w_b, m_b, v_b, t_b, maxpay = cross_active_inner(cur,A,U,c,b,w_b,m_b,v_b,t_b,lr_cross,beta_cross,inner_iters,active_tol,kmax,mix_y,N,n,sym)

### Downsample/upsample helpers
```
def resample_probability(a, n_new):
   old=a.size; if n_new==old return
   old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,old)
   new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_new)
   b=np.interp(new,old,a)
   b=np.maximum(b,0)
   s=b.sum(); if s<=0: uniform
   return b/s
```
For downsample maybe average blocks to preserve mass: if n_new < old, use interp (smooth). Good.

### Exact score function
```
def exact_score_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return inf
    a=np.where(finite,a,0); a=max(a,0)
    s=sum(a); if s<1e-12: set
    b=np.convolve(a,a)
    mx=max(b)
    val=2*n*mx/(s*s)
    return val if finite else inf
```
If n large and time, use. The evaluator clamps values to 1000; our final scaled values <1000. Good.

### finalize_sequence
Scale to sum sqrt(2n) to match SOTA. Ensure no `max(0)` issues. We can also sym final if score improved? We'll choose best_final array; before final, compare symmetric version using FFT or exact if time. At final:
```
def finalize_sequence(a):
   a=normalize_prob(a)
   n=a.size
   scale=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
   a=a*scale
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=sum(a)
   if s < 0.01: a*=0.01/max(s,1e-300)
   # maybe re-normalize to exact scale? Clipping unlikely.
   return [float(x) for x in a]
```
If we scale to sqrt(2n), sum exact scale; objective same. If values all <1000, no clip. For n=16384 scale=181; max prob maybe? Could some prob >5.5 to exceed 1000? To have max prob 5.5, sum squares central? objective huge. Not. Safe.

def make_cos(n, a1,a2,a3):
   t = (np.arange(n)- (n-1)/2) / max(1.0, (n-1)/2)
   base = 1 + a1*np.cos(np.pi*t) + a2*np.cos(2*np.pi*t) + a3*np.cos(3*np.pi*t)
   base=max(base,1e-3)
   return normalize_prob(base)
```

### Let's reason about correctness of gradient formulas for cross subproblem with pay offset c
The subproblem: minimize F(b)=smoothmax( (a*b)_active - c_active ). In cross inner, `base=c[idx]`, `h[idx]=a*b`, `pay=h-base`. Weight y. Gradient wrt b_j = ∑ y_k a_{k-j} = G_j. We compute G using p=y at active and U from A (a). Good. No factor 2. Candidate mix: new a=(1-t)a+t b. Coefficient k: c_new = c + 2t(1-t)(a*b)_k + t^2 b*b_k. First derivative =2(a*b - a*a) =2(a*b - c). Good. If pay negative, derivative negative.

**Self gradient**: minimize smoothmax(c_k). Gradient 2G with p. Good.

**Deconv multiplicative update**: Not strictly gradient, heuristic.

### Potential bug: In cross inner, `U` is from current `a`. If `a` normalized sum1. Good. But if `b` update, `B` computed; `h` = irfft(A*B) length L. For full convolution a*b, yes. If b sum1. If b has floor entries, sum1. Good.

### Active set selection and base c
`base=c[idx]` from current a. During inner, c active fixed. Good. If c contains values below due to renormalization? okay.

### If `pay` has large positive due to b far, weights focus. If `mx` small, division okay. If mx=0 (all zeros) impossible because sum1. But active maybe.

### Adam state across cross inner and outer
If cross outer fails, we still have b state from last inner. Next outer we might reuse b? But active set changed; better reset b to cur. In main after cross, if accepted reset b=cur; if rejected should we keep b? Maybe reset to cur to avoid weird. We'll reset b to cur and b states after every outer? That wastes inner progress? For cross, inner state matters within outer across iterations. After an outer, if no accept, reset b to cur for next outer. If accept, reset to new cur. Good. But inner iterations within outer update b; we need keep across those. So after calling cross_active_inner, outer done; reset regardless. That means each outer gets fresh b from current. Inner 10 steps enough. Good. It also simplifies. The adaptive beta/lr changes.

Similarly self state m_a/v_a can persist across self moves; but if current changes reset. For cross, no persistent between outers? Could keep, but not necessary.

Function `cross_active_inner` will update and return b states; main discards if reset. But cost of resetting means each outer 10 inner steps; okay. We can reduce inner if cross fails. Maybe keep state to leverage previous b? But active changes; resetting safe.

### Random restart in optimize
```
def random_restart_w(rng, base_w, n, sigma):
   w=base_w.copy()
   # smooth noise
   for r in range(1,6):
      amp = sigma / (r+1) * abs(rng.normal())
      ph = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
      x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
      w += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*r*x + ph)
   w += rng.normal(0, sigma*0.2, n)
   w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0)
   w = w - np.max(w)
   return w
```
When restart, choose base from top random (maybe best). Generate w, a=prob_from_log(w), sym alternative, score. Set cur to best of a/sym/best. Reset states.

LP formulation:
minimize t
s.t. (a*b)_k - c_k <= t for k in active
sum b=1
b>=0
We want t as low as possible. Variables [b_0..b_{n-1}, t]. t unbounded below; linprog requires bounds; set t lower -1, upper 1 (mx). Payoff values scale mx.
Objective c_obj: zeros for b, 1 for t.
A_ub rows: for each active k: coefficients cost_i = a_{k-i} (valid) for b, -1 for t (since cost - t <=0?) We need cost_i*b <= t + c_k => cost_i*b - t <= c_k. Thus A_ub row: cost, -1 (for t), b_ub=c_k.
Equality: sum b =1: row of ones for b, 0 for t.
Bounds: b [0,inf], t [-1,1].
Method 'highs'. Objective min t. If optimal t < -epsilon, b reduces active. However linprog with 4807 vars, 17 constraints, 4806 bounds, might be okay but each call maybe 0.1-1s. Repeating few could improve. But the LP minimizes max pay linearized; after mixing t (step) reduces. It may give better b than smooth Adam. ```
def solve_active_lp(a, c, idx, n):
   L=2*n-1
   K=idx.size
   A_ub = np.empty((K, n+1))
   for r,k in enumerate(idx):
       row=np.zeros(n+1)
       # cost_j = a_{k-j} if 0<=k-j<n
       # vector length n: for j where 0<=k-j<n => j in [k-n+1, k]
       lo=max(0, k-n+1); hi=min(n-1, k)
       if hi>=lo:
           row[lo:hi+1] = a[k:lo-1:-1]? Need vector cost_j = a_{k-j}. For j=lo..hi, k-j ranges hi? Let's create general:
           js=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
           row[js] = a[k-js]
       row[n] = -1.0
       A_ub[r]=row
       A_ub[r,n+1]? Wait columns n b + 1 t, total n+1. row[n] t.
   b_ub = c[idx]
   A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[:n]=1
   b_eq=[1]
   bounds=(0,None) for b + (-1,1) for t
   res=linprog(c_obj=zeros(n+1); c_obj[n]=1, A_ub=A_ub,b_ub=b_ub,A_eq=A_eq,b_eq=b_eq,bounds=bounds,method='highs')
   if res.success and res.x[-1] < 0:
       b=res.x[:n]; normalize; return b, res.x[-1]
   return None,None
```

### Detailed cross_active_inner implementation with returned maxpay
```
def cross_active_inner(a, A, U, c, b, w_b, m_b, v_b, t_b, lr, beta, inner, active_tol, kmax, mix_y, N, n):
    L=2*n-1
    mx=float(c.max())
    if mx <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(mx):
       return b,w_b,m_b,v_b,t_b,0.0
    thr=mx*(1.0-active_tol)
    idx=np.where(c >= thr)[0]
    amax=int(np.argmax(c))
    if amax not in idx:
       idx=np.concatenate((idx,np.array([amax],dtype=int)))
    if idx.size > kmax:
       vals=c[idx]
       kk=kmax
       part=np.argpartition(vals, -kk)[-kk:]
       idx=idx[part]
    if idx.size < 4:
       kk=min(4,L)
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -kk)[-kk:]
    base=c[idx]
    last_maxpay=0.0
    P=np.zeros(L)
    for it in range(inner):
       B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
       h=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)[:L]
       h=np.where(np.isfinite(h),h,0.0)
       h=np.maximum(h,0.0)
       pay=h[idx]-base
       maxpay=float(np.max(pay)) if pay.size else 0.0
       last_maxpay=maxpay
       if mx > 0:
          z=(pay-maxpay)/mx
          z=np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
          z=np.clip(beta*z, -60.0, 0.0)
       else: z=np.zeros(pay.size)
       y=np.exp(z)
       s=float(y.sum())
       if s > 0 and np.isfinite(s): y=y/s
       else: y=np.ones(pay.size)/max(1,pay.size)
       if mix_y > 0 and y.size:
          y = (1.0-mix_y)*y + mix_y/float(y.size)
          s=float(y.sum());
          if s>0: y/=s
       P.fill(0.0)
       P[idx]=y
       Pfft=np.fft.rfft(P,N)
       G=np.fft.irfft(Pfft*U,N)[n-1:2*n-1]
       G=np.where(np.isfinite(G),G,0.0)
       gw=b*(G - float(np.dot(b,G)))
       delta,m_b,v_b,t_b=adam_delta(gw,m_b,v_b,t_b,lr,0.5)
       w_b -= delta
       b=prob_from_log(w_b)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
          b=a.copy(); w_b=log_from_prob(a); break
    return b,w_b,m_b,v_b,t_b,last_maxpay
```
If idx is array of ints, `amax not in idx` works but O(K). Good.

### self_gradient_step
```
def self_gradient_step(a,A,U,c,mx,w_a,m_a,v_a,t_a,lr,beta,N,n):
    p=soft_top_weights(c,mx,beta=beta,uni_mix=0.15)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    G=2.0*np.fft.irfft(P*U,N)[n-1:2*n-1]
    G=np.where(np.isfinite(G),G,0.0)
    gw=a*(G - float(np.dot(a,G)))
    delta,...
    w_a -= delta
    a_new=prob_from_log(w_a)
    return a_new,w_a,m_a,v_a,t_a
```
Note w_a parameter modified in-place? We do `w_a = w_a - delta`, returns. Good.

### find_best_mixin
```
def find_best_mixin(a,b,base_score,N,n,tuple):
    best_a=None; best_score=base_score
    for tval in tuple:
       if tval <=0: cand=a
       elif tval>=1: cand=b
       else: cand=(1-tval)*a+tval*b
       cand=normalize_prob(cand)
       sc=fft_score_only(cand,N,n)
       if sc < best_score - 1e-14: best_score=sc; best_a=cand
       # sym
       cand2=symmetrize(cand); cand2=normalize_prob(cand2)
       # To avoid double if cand already symmetric, check maybe np.allclose? Use score only.
       sc2=fft_score_only(cand2,N,n)
       if sc2 < best_score -1e-14: best_score=sc2; best_a=cand2
    return best_a,best_score
```
If b equals a, sym. Good. We can save time: if tval==1 and cand already sym? Not needed.

### deconv_self_candidate
```
def deconv_self_candidate(a,N,phase,iters,mix,target_type,target_m,n):
    b=a.copy()
    L=2*n-1
    for it in range(iters):
       B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
       y=np.fft.irfft(B*B,N)[:L]
       y=np.where(np.isfinite(y),y,0.0)
       y=np.maximum(y,1e-300)
       m=float(y.max())
       if target_type ==0:
          t=1.0/L
       elif target_type ==1:
          t=make_clipped_target(y,target_m)
       elif target_type ==2:
          # windowed
          k=np.arange(L,dtype=float)
           center=(L-1)/2.0
          if center >0:
             x=(k-center)/center
             shape=1.0 - 0.6*x*x
             shape=np.maximum(shape,0.02)
          else: shape=np.ones(L)
          s=shape.sum(); t=shape/s
       else: t=1.0/L
       r=t/y
       mr=float(np.mean(r))
       if mr > 0 and np.isfinite(mr): r=r/mr
       r=np.where(np.isfinite(r),r,0.0)
       P=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
       U=phase*np.conjugate(B)
       corr=np.fft.irfft(P*U,N)[n-1:2*n-1]
       corr=np.where(np.isfinite(corr),corr,0.0)
       corr=np.maximum(corr,0.0)
       b_new=b*corr
       s=float(b_new.sum())
       if s>0 and np.isfinite(s): b_new=b_new/s
       else: b_new=b.copy()
       if mix <1.0:
          b_new=(1.0-mix)*b + mix*b_new
       b=normalize_prob(b_new)
    return b
```
For target_type 2, window max 1, normalized sum1, max ~1/(2n) much lower than target; okay as guide maybe too low. Could scale shape max to target_m? We can after normalize, scale so max = target_m if target_m>0 and sum? Not. Leave.

### make_clipped_target
```
def make_clipped_target(y,m_cap):
   L=y.size
   if m_cap <=0 or not finite: return ones/L
   t=np.minimum(y,m_cap)
   s=t.sum()
   if s <=0: return ones/L
   if s < 1.0:
      deficit=1.0-s
      room=m_cap - t
      room=np.maximum(room,0.0)
      cap=float(room.sum())
      if cap > deficit:
         t += (deficit/cap)*room
      else:
         return np.ones(L)/L
   s=f(t.sum())
   if s <=0: return ones/L
   t=t/s
   # ensure positive finite
   return np.where(np.isfinite(t),t,0.0)
```
If after normalization max maybe > m_cap? Since if s<1 we filled to max cap; if s>1 scaling down; max ≤ cap. Good.

### solve_active_lp
Need import linprog. Implement with top K.
```
def solve_active_lp(a,c,idx,n):
    if linprog is None: return None,None
    K=idx.size
    if K==0 or n>12000: return None,None
    A=np.zeros((K,n+1))
    b_ub=np.zeros(K)
    for i,k in enumerate(idx):
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       if hi>=lo:
          A[i,lo:hi+1] = a[k:lo-1:-1]
       A[i,n] = -1.0
       b_ub[i]=c[k]
    A_eq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
    c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[n]=1.0
    bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n
    bounds.append((-1.0,1.0))
    try:
       res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=[1.0], bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': 20})
    except Exception:
       return None,None
    if res is not None and res.success:
       b=res.x[:n]
       tval=float(res.x[n])
       b=np.where(np.isfinite(b),b,0.0)
       b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
       s=b.sum()
       if s>0 and np.isfinite(s):
          b=b/s
          return b,tval
    return None,None
```
`[1.0]` okay. `bounds` list of tuples. For n=9000, K=24, A ~1.9MB. Good.
Potential issue: `A_eq` variable name conflicts with array? fine. Use `Aeq`.

**Smooth log noise**
```
def smooth_log_noise(rng,w,sigma):
   n=w.size
   out=w.copy()
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   for r in range(1,6):
      amp=sigma*abs(float(rng.normal()))/(r+1)
      ph=float(rng.uniform(0.0,2.0*np.pi))
      out += amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*r*x + ph)
   out += np.array([float(rng.normal(0.0, sigma*0.1)) for _ in range(n)])? Cannot list comprehension? It is okay but no lambda. Could use rng.normal(size=n)*sigma*0.1.
   out=np.where(np.isfinite(out),out,0.0)
   return out - float(np.max(out))
```
No lambdas.

def random_log_candidate(n,rng,sigma=1.0):
   w=rng.normal(0.0,sigma,size=n)
   w=smooth_log_noise(rng,w,sigma*0.3) # adds more? no need.
   return w

def get_sorted_top(top):

       for j in range(i+1,m):

def finalize_sequence(a):
    a=normalize_prob(a)
    n=a.size
    scale=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=a*scale
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if np.isfinite(s) and s>0 and abs(s-scale)>1e-9:
        a*=scale/s
        a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if a.sum() <0.01: a*=0.01/max(...)
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```

   if float(score) < worst_score:

### Let's write `find_best_mixin` with tuple possibly empty? We'll pass. If no improvement, best_a None.

```
def find_best_mixin(a,b,base_score,N,n,trial_ts):
    best_a=None
    best_score=base_score
    for tval in trial_ts:
       if tval <=0.0: cand=a.copy()
       elif tval >=1.0: cand=b.copy()
       else: cand=(1.0-tval)*a + tval*b
       cand=normalize_prob(cand)
       sc=fft_score_only(cand,N,n)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-14:
          best_score=sc; best_a=cand
       cand2=symmetrize(cand)
       cand2=normalize_prob(cand2)
       sc2=fft_score_only(cand2,N,n)
       if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score - 1e-14:
          best_score=sc2; best_a=cand2
    return best_a,best_score
```
If `cand` is copy? For t=1, b may be used elsewhere; copy not necessary but okay.

global_best=res_best; global_score=res_score
for item in res_top: global_top=add_candidate(global_top,item[1],item[0])

### Let's produce final code now. Need ensure indentation and no missing imports.

I'll now craft final code in final. But before final, let's re-evaluate a few formulas for gradient with p sum1 and log gradient.
For softmax a_i=e^{w_i}/Z. The gradient wrt w_j of Φ(a) is a_j(∑_i ∂Φ/∂a_i a_i - ∂Φ/∂a_j). We set `gw = a*(G - dot(a,G))`. This equals a_j(G_j - dot). But correct is a_j(dot - G_j) for minimization? Let's derive: a = softmax(w). ∂a_i/∂w_j = a_j(δ_{ij} - a_i). For objective Φ, ∂Φ/∂w_j = ∑_i ∂Φ/∂a_i a_j(δ_{ij} - a_i) = a_j(G_j - ∑_i G_i a_i). Yes `G_j - dot`. For minimization, update w = w - lr * grad_w = a_j(G_j - dot). If G_j high, grad_w positive, reduce w_j. Our `gw=a*(G-dot)`, `w -= delta`, delta positive if G_j > dot. Good. Previous code same. For cross objective gradient G for b; same.

For cross pay objective, gradient G_j (not 2). If G_j high, reduce b_j. Good.

For self objective, derivative of c_k wrt a_j =2 a_{k-j}. If p weights, G=2q. Update w = w - lr* a*(G-dot). Good.

### Cross inner `h` from `A*B`: coefficient a*b. `base=c[idx]` the current a*a. Good.

### The LP objective minimizes t with row cost*a? For b variable, payoff (a*b)_k. Coeff row cost_j = a_{k-j}. Good. The LP uses b_ub = c[k], so row dot <= c[k] + t? Wait A_ub row: cost*b - t <= c[k]. Then cost*b <= c[k]+t. Objective min t. If t negative, bound c[k]+t < c[k]. This matches minimizing max(cost*b - c). Good. If b=a, cost*b=c, row LHS c - t <= c => -t <=0 => t>=0, so t=0 feasible. LP optimizes lower t. Good.

After LP, if t* negative, for active, payoff ≤ c + t* = c - |t|, derivative negative. Good.

### Fine.
